provided always that the Contractor shall not be held liable for the cost\nof preplacement of pegs which:\na) Cannot reasonably be re-established in their original positions by reason of the finished\ndimensions of the permanent works; and\nb) The Contractor can prove beyond reasonable doubt to the satisfaction of the Engineer,\nwere disturbed, damaged or destroyed by others beyond his control.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 38 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nPS A 5.3 PROTECTION OF EXISTING STRUCTURES\nREPLACE: “Machinery and Occupational Safety Act, 1983, (Act No. 6 of 1983)” WITH:\n“Occupational Health and Safety Act, 1993 (Act No. 85 of 1993), as amended” AND INSERT\nTHE FOLLOWING AFTER “(Act No. 27 of 1956)”: “as amended.”\nPS A 5.4 PROTECTION OF OVERHEAD AND UNDERGROUND SERVICES\nREPLACE THE HEADING AND THE CONTENTS OF THIS SUB-CLAUSE WITH THE\nFOLLOWING:\nPS A 5.4 LOCATION AND PROTECTION OF EXISTING SERVICES\nPS A 5.4.1 LOCATION OF EXISTING SERVICES\nBefore commencing with any work in an area, the Contractor shall ascertain the presence and\nactual position of all services which can reasonably be expected by and experienced and\ncompetent Contractor to be present on, under, over or within the Site.\nWithout in any way limiting its liability in terms of the Conditions of Contract in relation to\ndamage to property and interference with services, the Contractor shall, in collaboration with\nthe Engineer, obtain the most up-to-date plans as are available, showing the positions of\nservices existing in the area where it intends to work. Neither the Employer nor the Engineer\noffer any warranty as to the accuracy or completeness of such plans and because services\ncan often not be reliably located from plans, the Contractor shall ascertain the actual location\nof services depicted on such plans by means of careful inspection of the Site and the provision\nand utilization of suitable detecting and testing equipment.\nThereafter, the Contractor shall, by the use of appropriate methodologies carefully expose the\nservices at such positions as are agreed to by the Engineer, for the purposes of verifying the\nexact location and position of the services. Where the exposure of existing services involves\nexcavation to expose underground services, the further requirements of Sub-Clauses 4.4 and\n5.1.2.2 of SABS 1200 D, as amended, shall apply.\nThe aforesaid procedure shall also be followed in respect of services not shown on the plans,\nbut which may reasonably be anticipated by an experienced Contractor to be present or\npotentially present on the site.\nAll services, the positions of which have been determined as aforesaid at the critical points,\nshall henceforth be designated as ‘Known Services’ and their positions shall be indicated by\nthe Contractor on a separate set of drawings, a copy of which shall be furnished to the\nEngineer without delay.\nAs soon as any service which has not been identified and located as described above is\nencountered on, under, over or within the Site, it shall henceforth be deemed to be ‘Known\nService’ and the aforesaid provisions pertaining to locating, verifying and recording its position\non the balance of the site shall apply. The Contractor shall notify the Engineer immediately\nany such service is encountered or discovered on the site.\nWhilst it is in possession of the site, the Contractor shall be liable for all loss of or damage as\nmay occur to:\na) ‘Known Services’ anywhere along the entire lengths of their routes, as may reasonably be\ndeducted from the actual locations at which their positions were verified as aforesaid, due\ncognizance being taken of such deviations in line and level which may reasonably be\nanticipated; and\nb) Any other service which ought reasonably to have been a ‘Known Service’ in accordance\nwith the provisions of this Clause; as well as for consequential damage, whether caused\ndirectly by the Contractor’s operations or by the lack of proper protection;\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 39 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nProvided always that the Contractor will not be held liable in respect of damages occurring to\nservices not being ‘Known Services’.\nNo separate payment will be made to the Contractor in respect of its costs of providing, holding\navailable on the site and utilizing the said detecting and testing equipment, nor for any costs\nincurred in preparing and submitting to the Engineer the drawings as aforesaid and these\ncosts shall be deemed included in the tendered Sum.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nPS A 5.4.2 PROTECTION DURING CONSTRUCTION\nThe Contractor shall take all reasonable precautions and arrange its operations in such a\nmanner as to prevent damage occurring to all ‘Known Services’ during the period which the\nContractor has occupation and/or possession of the site.\nServices left exposed shall be suitably protected from damage and in such a manner as will\neliminate any danger arising there from for the public and/or workmen, all in accordance with\nthe requirements of the prevailing legislation and related regulations.\nPS A 5.4.3 ALTERATIONS AND REPAIRS TO EXISTING SERVICES\nUnless the contrary is clearly specified or ordered, the Contractor shall not carry out alterations\nto existing services. When this is necessary, the Contractor shall inform the Engineer, who\nwill either make arrangements for such work to be executed by the owner of the service, or\ninstruct the Contractor to make such arrangements himself.\nWhen the Contractor damages existing services, he shall immediately inform the Engineer or\nthe relevant authority and obtain instructions as to who should carry out repairs. In urgent\ncases the Contractor shall take the necessary steps to minimize damage to and interruption\nof the service. No repairs of telecommunication cables or electric power lines and cables shall\nbe attempted.\nThe Employer will accept no liability for damages due to a delay in having such alterations or\nrepairs affected. The Contractor shall provide all reasonable opportunity, access and\nassistance to persons carrying out alterations or repairs of existing services.\nPS A 5.7 SAFETY\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Before acceptance of the Contract, an Occupational Health and Safety Management File\nmust be handed over to the Engineer, acceptable to Department of Labour and maintained\nfor the duration of the project.\nPursuant to the provisions of the Conditions of Contract and without it in any way limiting the\nContractor’s obligations there under, the Contractor shall at its own expense, except only\nwhere specific provisions, if any, is made in the Contract for reimbursement to the Contractor\nin respect of particular items.\na) Provide to its Employees on the site of the Works, all safety materials, clothing and\nequipment necessary to ensure full compliance with the provisions of the Occupational\nHealth and Safety Act (Act No, 85 of 1993) as amended, hereinafter referred to as the\nAct, at all times and shall institute appropriate and effective measures to ensure the proper\nusage of such safety materials, clothing and equipment, at all times; and\nb) Provide, install and maintain on all barricades, safety signage and other measures to\nensure the safety of workmen and all persons in, on and around the site, as well as the\ngeneral public; and\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 40 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nc) Implement on the Site of Works, such procedures and systems and keep all records as\nmay be required to ensure compliance with the requirements of the Act at all times; reasonably required by the Engineer. Unless otherwise indicated in the\nContract or instructed by the Engineer, such meetings shall be held at the Contractor’s offices\non the Site. At such monthly meetings, matters such as general progress on the Work, quality\nof work, problems, claims, payments and safety, etc. shall be discussed but no matters\nconcerning the day-to-day running of the Contract.”\nA 6 TOLERANCES\nADD THE FOLLOWING SUB-CLAUSE TO CLAUSE 6:\n“PS A 6.1 USE OF TOLERANCES\nNo guarantee is given that the full specified tolerances will be available independently of each\nother and the Contractor is cautioned that the liberal or full use of any one or more of the\ntolerances may deprive him of the full or any use of tolerances relating to other aspects of the\nwork.\nExcept where the contrary is specified, or when clearly not applicable, all quantities for\nmeasurement and payment shall be determined from the ‘authorized’ dimensions. These\nspecified dimensions or those shown on the Drawings or, if changed, as finally prescribed by\nthe Engineer, without any allowance for the specified tolerances. Except if otherwise\nspecified, all measurements for determining quantities for payment will be based on the\n‘authorized’ dimensions.\nIf the work is constructed in accordance with the ‘authorized’ dimensions plus or minus the\ntolerances allowed, the calculation of quantities will be based on the ‘authorized’ dimensions,\nregardless of the actual dimensions to which the work has been constructed.\nWhen the work is not constructed in accordance with the ‘authorized’ dimensions plus or minus\nthe tolerances allowed, the Engineer may nevertheless, at his sole discretion, accept the work\nfor payment. In such cases no payment shall be made for quantities of work or material in\nexcess of those calculated for the ‘authorized’ dimensions and where the actual dimensions\nare less than the ‘authorized’ dimensions minus the tolerance allowed, quantities for payment\nshall be calculated based on the actual dimensions as constructed.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 42 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nA 7 TESTING\nPS A 7.1 PRINCIPALS\nPS A 7.1.2 STANDARD OF FINISHED WORK NOT TO SPECIFICATION\nINSERT THE WORDS “or checks by an approved laboratory…” AFTER THE WORDS “Where\nthe Engineer’s checks …” IN THE FIRST LINE OF SUB-CLAUSE 7.1.2.\nPS A 7.2 APPROVED LABORATORIES\nREPLACE THE CONTENTS OF SUB-CLAUSE 7.2 WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“Unless otherwise specified in the relevant Specification or elsewhere in the Project\nSpecifications, the following shall be deemed to be approved laboratories in which design\nwork, or testing required in terms of a Specification for the purposes of acceptance by the\nEngineer of the quality of materials used and/or workmanship achieved, may be carried out:\na) Any testing laboratory certified by the South African National Accreditation System\n(SANAS) in respect of the nature and type of testing to be undertaken for the purposes of\nthe Contract;\nb) Any testing laboratory owned, managed or operated by the Employer or the Engineer;\nc) Any testing laboratory established and operated on the site by or on behalf of the\nEmployer or the Engineer.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 43 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSANS 1200 AB: ENGINEER'S OFFICE\nAB 3 MATERIALS\nPS AB 3.2 OFFICE BUILDINGS\nThe contractor shall provide one board room with a table and chairs to accommodate at least 10\npeople for site meeting purposes.\nAB 4 PLANT\nPS AB 4.1 TELEPHONE\nThe Contractor’s contract manager and site agent must have a cellular phone available as contact\nbetween him and the engineer. The site agent must always be available on his cellular phone\nexcept during long weekends and the Christmas break where special arrangements will be\nrequired.\nAB 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS AB 5.5 SURVEY ASSISTANTS\nTwo semi-skilled labourers with relevant experience will be required to assist the engineer if\nrequired.\nPS AB 5.6 SURVEY EQUIPMENT\nThe Contractor shall provide the following tested and approved survey equipment (a certificate\nwill be required) on site for the duration of the contract and for the use of the Engineer whenever\nneeded:\na) one automatic level plus tripod and level staff.\nb) one 5m and one 50m measuring tape\nc) Diverse surveyor’s necessities like paint, pegs, etc.\nThe above-mentioned equipment may by arrangement be shared between the Contractor and\nthe Engineer's representative. It must be maintained and kept in good working order for the\nduration of the contract.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 44 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSANS 1200 C: SITE CLEARANCE\nPS C 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS C 5.2 CUTTING OF TREES\nPS C 5.2.3 PRESERVATION OF TREES\nPS C 5.2.3.2 Individual Trees\nThe Contractor shall pay a penalty of R5,000 for each Kameeldoring (Acacia Erioloba) tree\ndamaged or removed by him without the written permission of the Engineer and permit issued\nto do so.\nPS C 5.4 GRUBBING\nGrubbing shall consist of the grubbing out of roots and stumps to a depth of at least 600 mm\nbelow cleared surface level.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 45 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSABS 1200 D: EARTHWORKS\nPS D 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS D 5.1.2 EXISTING SERVICES\nPS D 5.1.2.4 DETECTION, LOCATION AND EXPOSURE\nADD THE FOLLOWING SUB-CLAUSE TO D 5.1.2.2\nIf existing services are not shown on the drawings but the existence thereof can be reasonably\nexpected, the Contractor shall, in conjunction with all relevant authorities, determine the exact\ndepth and location of such services before the commencement of construction.\nAfter locating the exact position of services, whether indicated on the drawing or not, such\nservices shall be deemed to be known services and the contractor shall be liable for all costs\nand subsequently costs arising from the damage thereof as a result of the Contractor’s\nactivities. These services must also be indicated on the “As-built” drawings.\nPS D 5.2 METHODS AND PROCEDURES\nPS D 5.2.3 PLACING AND COMPACTION\nPS D 5.2.3.1 DETECTION, LOCATION AND EXPOSURE\nSUBSTITUTE THE FOLLOWING SUB-CLAUSE TO D 5.2.3.1\nThe material of each embankment shall, unless otherwise approved, be deposited in layers of\nthickness, before compaction, not exceeding 300mm.\nWith\nThe material of each embankment shall, unless otherwise approved, be deposited in layers of\nthickness, before compaction, not exceed 150mm.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 46 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSABS 1200 DB: EARTHWORKS (PIPE TRENCHES)\nDB 3 MATERIALS\nPS DB 3.1 CLASSES OF EXCAVATION\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“In case of hand excavation, the following classification method will be used:\nPS DB 3.1.1 SOFT EXCAVATION\nSoft excavation is classified as material that can be removed with a shovel and pick. Should\nthis material be subjected to a DCP test, the density will be such that penetration will not be\nless than 10 mm per impact for a layer of 150 mm thick.\nPS DB 3.1.2 INTERMEDIATE EXCAVATION\nIntermediate excavation is classified as material that can be removed with a pick and shovel\nwhere penetration of a DCP apparatus is less than 10 mm per impact.\nPS DB 3.1.3 HARD EXCAVATION\nHard excavation is classified as material that can only be removed with pneumatic equipment,\nedges, splitting or explosives.”\nPS DB 3.7 SELECTION\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Where suitable backfill material occurs in layers of 150 mm or more, this material should be\nseparated during excavation from unsuitable material and used for backfilling. Should this\nmaterial not be utilized in this manner, an estimate will be made of the available quantities and\ndeducted from the imported backfill material.”\nDB 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS DB 5.6 BACKFILLING\nPS DB 5.6.4 REMOVAL OF INTERMEDIATE AND HARD ROCK MATERIAL\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“It is the responsibility of the Contractor to flatten out any spoil heaps and to dump subsequent\nloads on top. The Contractor will not be allowed to just dump spoil material on the horizontal\nsurface.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 47 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSABS 1200 G: CONCRETE (STRUCTURAL)\nG 3 MATERIALS\nPS G 3.2 CEMENT\nPS G 3.2.2 ALTERNATIVE TYPES OF CEMENT\nREPLACE THE CONTENTS OF THIS SUB-CLAUSE WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“Only ordinary Portland cement shall be used.\nIf the Contractor wishes to use any other type of cement, he shall obtain the Engineer’s\nprior written approval.”\nPS G 3.2.3 STORAGE OF CEMENT\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Cement shall not be stored for longer than 12 (twelve) weeks without the Engineer’s\npermission.”\nPS G 3.4 AGGREGATES\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“All aggregates used must comply with SABS 1083. No aggregates with a shrinkage\nexceeding 130% will be allowed.”\nPS G 3.4.1 ADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE G 3.4.1:\n“PS G 3.4.1.1 COURSE AGGREGATES\nPS G 3.4.1.1.1 Coarse aggregates must comply with the 10% FACT requirement for durability.”\nPS G 3.4.1.1.2 The nominal aggregate size is the smaller of the 37,5 mm maximum particle size and\n25% of slab thickness.\nPS G 3.4.1.1.3 Should the nominal aggregate size exceed 26,5 mm, the coarse aggregate shall be a\nmixture of b-aggregate greater than 26,5 mm and an a-aggregate smaller than\n26,5 mm.”\nPS G 3.4.1.2 FINE AGGREGATES\nPS G 3.4.1.2.1 Fine aggregate may not contain silicone particles in excess of 40%.\nPS G 3.4.1.2.2 Should the FM of the fine aggregate vary by more than ± 0,2 during construction,\nmodifications to the mix design should be done.\nPS G 3.4.1.3 MIX DESIGN\nSpecial consideration should be given to the minimizing of bleeding during the mix\ndesign. If bleeding is foreseen, special attention should be given to the fine aggregate.\nPS G 3.4.1.4 ADMIXTURES\nThe use of admixtures should be limited. Should admixtures be utilized, special\nattention should be given to possible shrinkage. The uses of any admixtures are to be\napproved by the Engineer. See also G 3.5.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 48 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nG 4 PLANT\nPS G 4.1 GENERAL\nADD THE FOLLOWING NEW SUB-CLAUSE:\n“PS G 4.1.1 MINIMUM PLANT\nThe Contractor shall have the following minimum plant available and in sound working\norder:\na) 2 (Two) concrete mixers, each of sufficient capacity to complete a section of floor\nslabs between construction joints within 4 (four) hours without interruption.\nIf the plant used for placing concrete for the structure is electrically or\nmechanically powered, the Contractor shall also provide some other approved\nnon-electrical powered standby means for placing concrete at an adequate rate\nin the event of a power or mechanical failure of the main plant.\nWhen the Contractor elects to utilize a crane during the construction period, he\nshall communicate with the Engineer in good time to allow for such positioning of\nthe crane.”\nPS G 4.5 FORMWORK\nPS G 4.5.1 DESIGN\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“All formwork or scaffolding required for any part of the Works shall be designed by the\nContractor and before commencing with the erection of any formwork or scaffolding, the\nContractor shall submit the methods he proposes to use to the Engineer for approval.\nThe Engineer has the authority to order alterations to the design or the sizes of any part\nof the formwork or scaffolding. The Contractor shall check the safety and suitability of\nall such alterations. The fact that the Engineer has approved or altered any part of the\nformwork or scaffolding shall not be construes as relieving the Contractor of his\nresponsibility with regard to the strength and stability of the formwork or scaffolding.”\nPS G 4.5.3 TIES\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“No plugs, bolts, ties or clamps of any description used to hold the formwork will be\nallowed to project into or through the concrete unless expressly approved by the\nEngineer.\nOnly approved tie-rods consisting of solid rods (that remain embedded in the concrete)\nand with removable ends shall be used to hold the formwork of the walls. The\nremovable tie-rod ends shall facilitate removal without damage to the concrete and no\npermanently embedded parts of such tie-rods shall have less than 50 mm of cover to\nthe finished concrete surface.\nThe cavities left in the concrete when the tie-rod end cones are removed shall be\nsoundly caulked with a cement mortar to which an approved shrinkage-reducing agent\nhas been added and shall be neatly finished to a smooth surface uniform with that of\nthe surrounding concrete.\nThe cost of supplying special tie-rods as well as the filling of cavities left by the tie-rod\ncones shall be included in the tendered Sum.\nOn no account shall formwork be secured to reinforcing bars.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 49 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nG 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS G 5.1 REINFORCEMENT\nPS G 5.1.2 FIXING\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“The Engineer will inspect the reinforcing after it has been fixed in place, the formwork\nhas been cleaned, cover blocks have been positioned and before concreting\ncommences.\nWelding of reinforcing steel will not be permitted.”\nPS G 5.1.3 COVER\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“The distance between pipes in the concrete and the reinforcing steel shall nowhere be\nless than:\na) 40 mm or\nb) 5 mm plus the maximum size of the coarse aggregate, whichever is the\nlargest.”\nPS G 5.2 FORMWORK\nPS G 5.2.1 CLASSIFICATION OF FINISHES\nc) SPECIAL\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“This finish is obtained by first giving the surface a smooth finish with the joints between\nformwork panels forming an approved regular pattern suitable for the appearance of the\nstructure. All projections shall then be removed, irregularities repaired and the surface\nrubbed or otherwise treated until it is smooth with an even texture, appearance and\ncolour.\nIf the finish of exposed surfaces does not comply with the requirements for uniformity of\nthe texture and appearance, the Contractor shall, when instructed to do so by the\nEngineer, rub down the exposed surfaces of the entire structure or any part thereof as\nspecified below, entirely at his own cost. All repairs must be completed before the\nrubbing commences.\nThe surface shall be saturated with water for at least 1 (one) hour. The initial rubbing\nof the face shall be carried out with a medium coarse carborundum stone, together with\na small amount of mortar of the same cement/sand ratio as the concrete being repaired.\nRubbing shall continue until all form marks, projections and irregularities have been\nremoved and a uniform surface has been obtained. The paste produced by the rubbing\nshall be kept in place. The final rubbing shall be carried out with a fine carborundum\nstone and water. This rubbing shall continue until the entire surface has a smooth, even\ntexture and is uniform in colour. The surface shall subsequently be washed with a brush\nto remove surplus paste and powder.”\nPS G 5.3 HOLES, CHASE AND FIXING BLOCKS\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 50 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\n“Cover blocks for reinforcing and fixtures may be placed into the concrete provided that\nneither the strength nor any other desirable characteristic (such as the appearance) of\nthe concrete section is affected or impaired in the opinion of the Engineer.”\nPS G 5.5 CONCRETE\nPS G 5.5.1 QUALITY\nPS G 5.5.1.5 DURABILITY\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“The exposure conditions of the concrete are classified as ‘severe’.”\nPS G 5.5.1.7 STRENGTH CONCRETE\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“The concrete mixes for the works shall be designed by the Portland Cement Institute\nor a similar approved laboratory.”\nPS G 5.5.3 MIXING\nPS G 5.5.3.2 READY-MIX CONCRETE\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Ready-mixed concrete may be used on the site. All valves to be clockwise (right hand) closing and\nthe direction of opening and closing should be permanently displayed on the valve casing.\nValves should be of the non-rising spindle type and be equipped with a square cap-top suitable\nfor use with a valve spanner. All valves will be tested for water tightness. One valve spanner\nis to be provided for every 10 (ten) valves or less.”\nPS L 5.1.4 DEPTH AND COVER\nADD THE FOLLOWING NEW SUB-CLAUSE:\n“PS L 5.1.4.6 A minimum cover of 500 mm is required over the top of pipes inside erven; 800 mm in street\nreserves, underneath roads or as specified by levels on long sections and plans. Where\ninstructed, pipes are to be encased in concrete.”\nL 7 TESTING\nPS L 7.3 STANDARD HYDRAULIC PIPE TEST\nPS L 7.3.1 TEST PRESSURE AND TIME OF TEST\nPS L 7.3.1.1 TEST PRESSURE\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Before any connections are made, pipes are to be tested to 1.5 times the working pressure\nof the specific class of pipe. After connected to existing pipelines or fittings, all costs\nassociated with the excavation, removal of fittings, cutting in, joining, labour and complete\nfinishing are deemed to be included in the tendered price.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 53 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSABS 1200 LB: BEDDING PIPES\nLB 3 MATERIAL\nPS LB 3.1 SELECTED GRANULAR MATERIAL\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Notwithstanding the provisions in Sub Clause LB 3.1, the selected granular material shall be\nsingularly graded between 0,6 mm and 13 mm.”\nPS LB 3.2 SELECTED FILL MATERIAL\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Notwithstanding the requirements of Sub Clause LB 3.2, the selected fill material for storm\nwater pipelines in all areas, excluding roadways, shall have a PI not exceeding 18. This\namendment is not applicable to sewers and water mains.”\nPS LB 3.3 BEDDING\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Class C bedding as applicable to rigid pipes is required. Material for the Class C bedding will\nonly be imported where insufficient suitable material is obtainable from the excavated material.\nWhere large diameter UPVC pipes (> 300 mm diameter) are utilized, compaction on either\nside of the pipe should be carefully done in layers not exceeding 100 mm in thickness to\nensure that the bedding and pipe act as a “pipe-soil system” to prevent ovality of the pipes\noccurring during backfilling.\nThe Contractor shall be responsible for finding a source of suitable bedding material.”\nLB 3.4 SELECTION\nPS LB 3.4.2 SUITABLE MATERIAL NOT AVAILABLE FROM THE TRENCH EXCAVATION\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“Should there, during selective excavation methods with the correct tools, still be insufficient\nsuitable material available for the bedding, material must be imported. The Contractor will find\na suitable source of bedding material and submit it to the Engineer for approval.\nThe finding of a suitable source/quarry/ borrow pit, loading, placement and compaction of the\nimported material is deemed to be included in the rate tendered by the Contractor.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 54 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nLB 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS LB 5.1.4 COMPACTION\nADD THE FOLLOWING:\n“After excavation of the trench, the trench bottom will be levelled by means of a rake and\ncompacted. Compaction may be conducted by hand tools. The required compaction to be\nachieved must exceed or equal 90% mod AASHTO density.\nAfter installation of the pipes, similar compaction must be applied to the blanket material. Now\nthe final backfilling to 50 mm above the adjacent soil levels may be carried out. The total\nworking area shall then be finished off. Only when specified by the Engineer will sidewalks\nbe finished to specific standards and levels.\nWhere streets and roads are crossed, compaction of the bedding and backfill must be\nconducted by mechanical means to achieve a density of 98% mod AASHTO density.”\nPS LB 5.2.1(c) CLASS A BEDDING\nAMEND THE SUB-CLAUSE WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“The main fill shall not be placed in any section until the bedding cradle in that section has\nachieved a compressive strength of at least 15 MPa or a period of 5 days has elapsed after\nthe placing of the concrete in that section, whichever occurs first.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 55 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nSANS 1200 LD: SEWER\nLD 3 MATERIALS\nPS LD 3.5 MANHOLES, CHAMBERS, ETC.\nPS LD 3.5.2 PRE-CAST CONCRETE SECTIONS\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 3.5.2:\n“The joint between the manhole and the cement cover must be effectively sealed with a\nsealant so that no ground water or storm water can filter in. The lifting holes in the manholes\nmust be sealed with epoxy after installation but before back filling.\nBenching must be made from concrete fabricated from dolomitic aggregate.”\nPS LD 3.5.3 PRE-FABRICATED FC MANHOLES\nREPLACE SUB-CLAUSE LD 3.5.3 WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“Pre-fabricated FC-manholes must comply with the requirements of fibre strengthened cement\nsewer pipes according to SANS 819 with inside diameter of 1 000 mm. All manholes must be\ndipped in bitumen.\nThe joint between the manhole and the cement cover must be effectively sealed with a sealant\nso that no ground water or storm water can filter in. The lifting holes in the manholes must be\nsealed with epoxy after installation but before back filling.\nBenching must be made from concrete fabricated from dolomitic aggregate.”\nPS LD 3.5.7 STEP IRONS\nREPLACE SUB-CLAUSE LD 3.5.7 WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“Step irons shall be manufactured of at least 12 mm tensile strength armour-plated steel with\na polypropylene casing. Attaching of step irons in manholes must be done according to the\ninstructions and requirements of the Manufacturers.”\nPS LD 3.6 MARKER POSTS\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 3.6:\n“Marker posts consisting of a 1 m length kerbing that is planted vertically above the end cap\nof the erf connection. The end of the kerbing must be 400 mm above natural ground level and\npainted red, while the bottom is connected to the end cap with a 3 mm wire.”\nLD 5 CONSTRUCTION\nPS LD 5.4 CONNECTIONS TO MANHOLES\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 5.4:\n“Where a pipe lies at a gradient of 1:10 (that is 5,71°) an 11¼° bend cannot be used as a bend\nwith an angle greater than the gradient of the pipe will produce a low point. It is the\nContractor’s responsibility to shorten the bend to form the desirable angle.\nFor pipes with a gradient of 1:10 the angle can be taken up by a coupling in the manhole and,\nwhere necessary, also by a coupling between the short length and first full pipe.”\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 56 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nLD 5.6 MANHOLES, INSPECTION CHAMBERS, ETC.\nPS LD 5.6.1 GENERAL\nREPLACE SUB-CLAUSE LD 5.6.1(A) WITH THE FOLLOWING:\n“Manholes on new pipes shall be of FC, brick or concrete and built as indicated on the\ndrawings.\nFinal cover levels of manholes must be as follows:\n1. In roads and on pavements : level with the road or pavement\n2. All other areas : 50 mm above final level\nIf the position of a manhole is such that it is situated in a low or a hole where there is a danger\nof storm water filtering into the cover, the final level of the manhole must be elevated to such\na level where storm water infiltration is no longer a danger, or to a level previously approved\nby the Engineer.\nIf the manhole cover for FC or concrete manholes must be elevated with more than 300 mm,\nFC or concrete rings with the same diameter as the manhole, and attached to the fibre cement\nor concrete manhole with epoxy, must be used.”\nPS LD 5.6.2 BENCHING\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 5.6.2.3:\n“Benching will be constructed and finished off as indicated on the drawings.”\nPS LD 5.9 CONNECTING SEWERS\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 5.9.1:\n“Plot connections must be installed in the exact position as indicated on the drawings. Where\nmains go through plots the erf connection must end 1 m from where the manifold and 45°\nbend ends on the erf. Where mains are outside the plot the erf connection must be 1 m inside\nthe plot border, and sealed with an end cap.\nUnless stipulated differently on the drawings or specified by the Engineer, all connecting\nsewers must be laid with a gradient of 1:60.”\nLD 7 TESTING\nPS LD 7.1 GENERAL\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 7.1.5:\n“All tests shall be repeated after back filling of pipe trenches has been completed.”\nPS LD 7.2.6 WATERTIGHTNESS OF MANHOLES\nADD THE FOLLOWING TO SUB-CLAUSE LD 7.2.6:\n“On completion of a manhole, the Engineer might call for a water tightness test, at the\nContractor’s expense, as follows:\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 57 C 3.1\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Scope of Works:12/2026\n\nManholes must be filled to the brim with water and left to stand for 60 minutes. Loss must be determined with comparison 1, also see\nTable below.\nI = 0,6.D – (1)\nI = Water loss in l/hour/metre depth\nD = manhole diameter in m.\nShould the manhole leak more water than given in comparison 1, the manhole must be water\nproofed and re-tested. The Contractor shall pay for the water at the rates and tariffs\nas determined by the Local Authority, including the cost of supplying a temporary standpipe\nas required.\nC 3.4.3.2 SOURCE OF POWER SUPPLY\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements for obtaining power and be responsible for\nall costs involved.\nC 3.4.3.3 LOCATION OF CAMP AND DEPOT\nThe Contractor must make his own arrangements for a Camp Site at the location of the Works.\nThe location of the Contractor’s camp, including the material storage areas, will be subject to\nthe Engineer’s approval.\nThe Contractor shall make his own arrangements for the accommodation of labour.\nC 3.4.3.4 SPOIL SITES\nNo indiscriminate spoiling of material will be allowed.\nAll unsuitable surplus material shall be removed from the Site and the Contractor shall make\nhis own arrangements with regard to a suitable spoil site.\nC 3.4.4 ABNORMAL RAINFALL\nRefer to C 3.1.6.3.10\nC 3.4.5 TIME RELATED ITEMS\nAn approved extension of time (other than an extension of time granted in terms of the Special\nConditions of Contract) will entitle the Contractor to submit a claim for additional payment.\nAny such approved additional payment will be made for proven additional costs for each\nrelevant time related item.\nC 3.4.6 NAMEBOARD\nNo name board will be provided.\nC 3.4.7 PROTECTION FROM STORMS AND FLOODS\nThe tendered Sum shall be deemed to be full compensation for any damage to the Works due\nto storms, rain, floods, storm water or subsurface water.\nUnder no circumstances shall the Contractor be entitled to any additional payment in this\nregard. The Contractor shall accept full responsibility and costs to handle water from any\nsource on his Site.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 66 C 3.4\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Management: 12/2026\n\nC 3.4.8 EXISTING SERVICES\nThe Engineer will provide information regarding the location of existing utility services, but the\nEngineer does not accept responsibility for the accuracy of this information. The Contract\nshall make further investigations to determine the exact locality, size and depth of existing\nservices before commencing construction to ensure that no damage is done to any service.\nThe Contractor shall take all reasonable precautions to protect existing services during\nconstruction and during relocation of such services.\nAny pipe, cable, conduit or other services of any nature whatsoever indicated to the Contractor\nand subsequently damaged as a result of the Contractor’s operations shall be repaired and\nreinstated forthwith by the Contractor or by the authority concerned, all at the expense of the\nContractor and to the satisfaction of the Engineer.\nWhenever services are encountered which interfere with the execution of the works and which\nrequire to be moved and relocated, the Contractor shall advise the Engineer, who will\ndetermine the extent of the work, if any, to be undertaken by the Contractor in removing,\nrelocating and reinstating such services.\nThe Contractor shall work in close co-operation with private owners or public authorities\ncontrolling services, which have to be protected, removed or relocated. No undertaking can\nbe given as to the exact time of commencement or of completion of the relocation, removal or\nprotection of services, which have to be carried out by the owners or controlling authorities\nthemselves. The Contractor is to make allowance in his programme for this contingency.\nWhere services have to be removed or relocated or protected, the Engineer will at the request\nof the Contractor, notify or negotiate with the owners or Authorities controlling those services,\nbut the Engineer does not accept liability for any costs resulting from delays in the relocation,\nremoval or protection of any service, or delays as a result of delays in negotiations.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nC 3.4.9 ACCOMMODATION OF TRAFFIC AND PUBLIC ACCESS\nDuring all his operations and when using his machinery, plant and equipment, the Contractor\nshall at all times take the necessary cate to protect the public and to facilitate the traffic flow.\nC 3.4.10 SETTING OUT OF WORKS\nAll setting out required to carry out the work shall be undertaken by the Contractor. Setting\nout of the Works to be included in the tendered Sum.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nC 3.4.11 SANITARY CONDITIONS\nThe Contractor shall ensure that, during the period of construction, sanitary conditions prevail\non the site and surrounding areas. Unhygienic behaviour that may cause contamination of\nthe works or the surrounding area is strictly prohibited.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 67 C 3.4\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Management: 12/2026\n\nC 3.4.12 CONSTRUCTION IN CONFINED AREAS\nIt may be necessary for the Contractor to work within confined areas and no additional\npayment will be made for work done in restricted areas. The method of construction in these\nconfined areas will depend largely on the Contractor’s construction plant.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nC 3.4.13 DENSITY TESTS / CONCRETE CUBES\nThe Contractor shall carry out his own density tests on each compacted layer and these tests\nshall be submitted to the Engineer for scrutiny and approval before commencing with the\nconstruction of the following layer.\nThe Contractor also needs to do his own concrete cube tests, which is to be handed to the\nEngineer for scrutiny and approval. The Engineer may order that further, control tests are to\nbe taken.\nThe Engineer may order that control tests be taken by his own or another independent\nlaboratory. Cube/density tests carried out by the Contractor in the normal course of his work\nshall be carried out at his own expense.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nC 3.4.14 HEALTH AND SAFETY SPECIFICATION\nC 3.4.14.1 PURPOSE\nIn terms of the Occupational Health and Safety Act (Act 85 of 1993) (OHSA) or as amended,\nand the Construction Regulations 2014 or as amended, the Employer must provide the\nContractor with a Health and Safety Specification to which the Contractor must respond with\na Health and Safety Plan for approval by the Employer.\nThe purpose of this Specification is to ensure that the Principal Contractors entering into a\ncontract with the Employer maintain an acceptable level of performance with regard to health\nand safety issues during the performance of the contract. In this regard the OHSA\nSpecification form an integral part of the Contract and the Principal Contractor shall ensure\nthat their Contractors and/or Suppliers comply with the requirements of this Specification.\nC 3.4.14.2 SCOPE\nThis Contract comprises of THE CONSTRUCTION OF 69 BNG HOUSES IN NABABEEP,\nNAMA-KHOI LOCAL MUNICIPALITY FOR THE NAMAKWA DISTRICTMUNICIPALITY.\nThe Contractor, in complying with the OHS Act and the Construction Regulations, shall\nconsider all aspects of the Works described and take into account the construction methods\nand materials to be used.\nC 3.4.14.3 GENERAL\nThe Contractor is referred to and shall comply with the full text of the Occupational Health and\nSafety Act (Act 85 of 1993) (OHSA) or as amended and to the Construction Regulations 2014\nor as amended, promulgated there under.\nIn this regard refer also to the Health and Safety Agreement and Conditions attached to these\nContract documents (See Part C 1.4)\nThe following Specification covers health and safety matters applicable during construction.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 68 C 3.4\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Management: 12/2026\n\nAll the work included in this Contract shall, for the purpose of complying with OHSA and the\nConstruction Regulations, be deemed to be “construction work”.\nIt should be noted that, with a few exceptions, the Model Preambles and the Project\nSpecifications are “end product specifications” and not “method Specifications”. As the\nmethods of construction to be used are generally determined by the Contractor, detailed safety\nrequirements applicable to all the operations to be carried out on Site are not provided in the\nproject documentation. The Contractor shall apply all the relevant regulations and\nrequirements to the work methods and materials used.\nThe Principal Contractor shall give the required notice to the Provincial Department of Labour\nbefore commencement of any work on Site. This notice shall include the information as\nrequired by the Construction Regulations and shall be signed by the Contractor and the\nEmployer.\nThe Principal Contractor shall ensure current registration and good standing with the\nCompensation Commissioner and shall provide evidence to this effect to the Employer.\nIt is the responsibility of the Principal Contractor and his Contractors to provide for all costs\nand expenses related to the management of and compliance with the OHSA and this\nSpecification.\nIt will be accepted that the Tenderer made provision in his tendered Sum for the cost\nof the above. No additional payment will be made by the Client.\nC 3.4.14.4 EXISTING SITE CONDITIONS\nThe Contractor shall take into account, inter alia, the following existing conditions when\ncomplying with the OHSA:\na) Existing utility services;\nb) Existing site conditions. The Contractor shall be deemed to have visited the site and\nexamined the site conditions applicable for the Works;\nc) The traffic accommodation requirements;\nd) Surrounding land use;\ne) Anticipated weather conditions for the area; and\nf) Access to the public and the use of the facility during construction.\nC 3.4.14.5 DESIGN INFORMATION\nDesign information provided for safety planning purposes, such as design loads for structures,\nfoundation conditions, etc. is available from the Engineer where required.\nC 3.4.14.6 CONSTRUCTION MATERIALS\nThe following commonly used construction materials and substances potentially pose health\nand safety hazards:\na) All materials contained in pressurized containers;\nb) Bitumen and tar products;\nc) Cement;\nd) Epoxies;\ne) Lime and other stabilizing agents;\nf) Paints;\ng) Timber preservatives; and\nh) Asbestos cement products.\nPart C 3: Scope of Work C 3 - 69 C 3.4\nTender Number: NC/12/2026 Management: 12/2026\n\nThe materials to be used to construct the Works are described in the following:\na) The Project Specification\nThe Contractor shall take appropriate measures to manage the risks associated with the use\nof all materials required to complete the “Works, i.e. not only those listed above, and shall,\ninter alia, implement all the precautionary measures provided by Manufacturers and Suppliers\nfor the storage, use and application of materials used.\nC 3.4.14.7 SITE ACCESS AND ENVIRONMENTAL CONDITIONS\na) Site access, egress, deliveries and vehicular and pedestrian routes\nThe requirements regarding the control of access to and egress from the Site and\nvehicular and pedestrian routes are to be noted by the Contractor and provision is to be\nmade to ensure the safety of all pedestrians and vehicular traffic at all times.\nb) Environment\nThe Contractor shall ensure compliance with all current environmental legislation\napplicable to the Works and the Site. The Contractor is advised of the existing asbestos\ncement products and all necessary environmental precautions and requirements shall be\nadhered to in this regard.\nC 3.4.14.8 USE OF SITE BY THE EMPLOYER\nAny continues use of the Site required by the Employer to maintain traffic flows or to allow\nwork to be done by other Contractors or Authorities is a requirement of this contract and the\nContractor shall take due precaution in this regard.\nC 3.4.14.9 SITE RULES\na) Way leaves, permissions and permits:\nThe Contractor shall be responsible for obtaining all the way leaves, permissions or\npermits applicable to working near any existing services or other infrastructure on Site and\nshall abide by the safety conditions imposed by such way leaves, permissions or permits.\nb) Reporting of incidents:\nAll incidents shall be reported strictly in accordance with the requirements of the OHSA\nand the General Conditions of Contract.\nC 3.4.14.10 HEALTH AND SAFETY PLAN\nIn compliance with the Construction Regulations the Contractor shall, after performing a risk\nassessment, prepare a Health and Safety Plan for approval by the Employer.\nThe Health and Safety Plan shall include, but not be limited to, the following:\na) The Safety Management Structure, including the names of all designated persons such\nas the Construction Supervisor and any other competent persons;\nb) Safety Method Statements and procedures to be adopted to ensure compliance with the\nOHSA. NC/12/2026\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF 69 BNG HOUSES IN NABABEEP, NAMA-KHOI LOCAL\nMUNICIPALITY FOR THE NAMAKWA DISTRICT MUNICIPALITY\n1. HEALTH AND SAFETY SPECIFICATION\n1.1 Scope\nThis Health & Safety Specification has been developed to address all aspects of occupational\nhealth and safety, as affected by the proposed construction work in accordance with the provisions\nin the Construction Regulations.\nThe specification provides the requirements that the Principal Contractor and other Contractors\nshall have to comply with to reduce the risks associated with the construction work to a level as\nlow as reasonably practicable.\n1.2 Introduction\nIn terms of Construction Regulation 5(1) (b) and (c) of the Occupational Health and Safety Act,\nNo. 85 of 1993, the Client, or his Health and Safety Agent, is required to compile a Site-Specific\nHealth & Safety Specification for any intended project and provide such specification to the\nDesigner as well as to any prospective tenderers.\nThe Client's further duties are stipulated in Clause 3, and in the Construction Regulations,\npublished in Government Gazette No 37305 of 2014. This specification has an objective to ensure\nthat Principal Contractors and other Contractors entering in to a Contract with the Client, achieve\nan acceptable level of Occupational Health & Safety performance. This document forms an\nintegral part of the Contract and Principal Contractors should make it part of any Contracts that\nthey may have with Contractors and/or Suppliers.\nCompliance with this document does not absolve the Principal Contractor and other Contractors\nfrom complying with minimum legal requirements. All Contractors remain responsible for the\nhealth & safety of his employees, persons other than his employees in terms of Section 9 of the\nOccupational Health and Safety Act, No. 85 of 1993 and those of his Mandatory’s\n1.3 General Occupational Health and Safety Provisions\n1.3.1 Hazard Identification & Risk Assessment\n1.3.1.1 Development of Risk Assessments\nEvery Contractor shall appoint a competent person in writing to perform a Risk Assessment before\nthe commencement of any Construction work. This Risk Assessment shall form part of the\nOccupational Health and Safety Plan and be implemented and maintained as contemplated in\nConstruction regulation 5(1).\nThe Risk Assessment shall include at least the following:\n• the identification of the risks and hazards to which persons may be exposed to\n• the analysis and evaluation of the identified risks and hazards\n• a documented plan of safe work procedures to mitigate, reduce or control the risks and\nhazards that have been identified\n• a monitoring plan, and\n• a review plans\n• material safety data sheets\nBased on the Risk Assessments, the Contractor must develop a set of site-specific Occupational\nHealth & Safety rules that will be applied to regulate the Occupational Health & Safety aspects of\n\nthe construction. The Risk Assessments, together with the site-specific Occupational Health &\nSafety rules shall be submitted to the Client before mobilisation on site commences.\nThe Contractor is required to conduct a baseline Risk Assessment of the risks he anticipates\nencountering during the project. The baseline Risk Assessment must include the Standard\nWorking Procedures (SWP) and the applicable Method Statements based on the Risk\nAssessments.\n1.3.1.2 Review of Risk Assessments\nThe Contractor is to review the Hazard Identification, Risk Assessments and Safe Work\nProcedure's at each Production Planning and Progress Report meeting as the Contract work\ndevelops and progresses and each time changes are made to the designs, plans and construction\nmethods and processes [monthly].\nThe Contractor shall provide the Client, other Contractors and all other concerned-parties with\ncopies of any changes, alterations or amendments brought about by the above.\n1.3.2 Legal Requirements\nAll Contractors entering into a Contract with the Client, shall, as a minimum, comply with the:\n• Occupational Health & Safety Act and Regulations (Act 85 of 1993). A current, up-to-date\ncopy of the Occupational Health Safety Act shall be available on site always.\n• Compensation for Occupational Injuries & Diseases Act (Act 130 of 1993). The Principal\nContractor will be required to submit a letter of Registration and "good-standing" from the\nCompensation Insurer before being awarded the Contract. A current, up-to-date copy of\nthe Compensation for Occupational Injury and Diseases Act (COIDA) shall be\navailable on site at all times.\n• The Client must determine the competency of Contractors/persons he allows (authorise) to\nenter such premises.\n1.3.3 Structure and Responsibilities\n1.3.3.1 Overall Supervision and Responsibility for Occupational Health and Safety\n- The Client is to ensure that the Contractor, appointed in terms of Construction Regulation\n5(1) (k), implements and maintains the agreed and approved Occupational Health & Safety\nPlan.\n- The Chief Executive Officer of the Contractor, in terms of Section 16(1) of the Act, is to\nensure that the Employer (as defined in the Act) complies with the Act. Annexure 5. "Audit\nSystem" may be used for this purpose.\n- It is a requirement that the Contractor, when he appoints Contractors in terms of\nConstruction Regulations 7 includes an Occupational Health & Safety Act Section 37(2)\nagreement (“Agreement with Mandatory”) in his agreement with such Contractors.\n- Every project must have an Occupational Health & Safety Act (85 /1993), Section 16(2)\nAppointee.\n- The client must ensure that the contractor appoints a Construction Supervisor and Assistant\nConstruction Supervisor in terms of Construction Regulation 8(8).\n1.3.3.2 Further (Specific) Supervision Responsibilities for Occupational Health & Safety\nThe Contractor shall appoint designated competent employees and/or other competent persons\nas required by the Act and Regulations. The appointments shall be in writing and the\nresponsibilities clearly stated together with the period for which the appointment is made. This\ninformation must be communicated and agreed with the appointees.\n\n1.3.3.3 Designation of Occupational Health & Safety Representatives (Section 18 of the\nOccupational Health & Safety Act)\nThe Contractors shall ensure Occupational Health & Safety Representatives are appointed for\nevery workplace where employees (including the employees of other Contractors) are exposed to\nrisk.\nOccupational Health & Safety Representatives have to be designated in writing and the\ndesignation must include the area of responsibility of the person and term of the designation.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that the designated OH&S Representatives conduct an inspection of\ntheir respective areas of responsibility using a checklist and report thereon.\nOccupational Health & Safety representatives shall be included in accident/incident investigations\nand must attend all Occupational Health & Safety committee meetings.\n1.3.4 Administrative Controls and the Occupational Health & Safety File\n1.3.4.1 The Occupational Health & Safety File\nAs required by Construction Regulation 7(1)(b), the Principal Contractor and other Contractors\nwill each keep an updated Occupational Health & Safety File on site containing the following\ndocuments as a minimum:\n• Permit to construct Notification of Construction Work (Construction Regulations 4)\n• Copy of Occupational Health & Safety Act (updated) (Gen Administrative Regulation 4)\n• Proof of Registration and good standing with a COID Insurer (Construction Regulation\n5(1) (j)\n• Occupational Health & Safety Programme/Plan agreed with the Client including the\nunderpinning Risk Assessment/s & Method Statements (Construction Reg 5(1)(q)\n• Copies of Occupational Health & Safety Committee and other relevant Minutes\n• Designs/drawings\n• A list of Contractors including copies of the agreements between the parties (Section\n37(2) agreement in terms of the OHS act) and the type of work being done by each\nContractor\n• Appointment/Designation forms (For example H&S rep, first aider etc.)\n• Electrical Installations, -Equipment & -Appliances including temporary certificate of\ncompliance\n• All other applicable records\n1.3.5 OH&S Goals & Objectives & Arrangements for Monitoring & Review of\nOccupational Health and Safety Performance\nThe Contractor is required to report all incidents to the Project Manager/Client. The Project\nmanager must also submit an up to date report regarding all incidents to the Head, OHS.\n1.3.6 Notification of Construction Work\nThe Contractors shall, where the Contract meets the requirements laid down in Construction\nRegulation 4, notify the Department of Labour at least 7 days before the commencement of work\nof the intention to carry out construction work.\nA copy must be held on the Occupational Health & Safety File and included into the project file.\n\n1.3.7 Training, Awareness and Competence\n1.3.7.1 General Induction Training\nAll persons on site are to attend a general induction session presented by the Contractor.\nAll persons on the site shall be in possession of documentation/proof that they have undergone\nGeneral Induction training.\nThe Contractor will be required to develop project specific induction training based on the Risk\nAssessments for the Contract work and train all employees and other Contractors and their\nemployees in this.\n1.3.7.2 Other Training\nAll operators, drivers and users of construction vehicles, mobile plant and other equipment (for\nexample overhead cranes) shall be in possession of documentation proving that they have\nundergone training to operate said vehicles, plant and equipment.\nAll employees in jobs requiring training in terms of the Act and Regulations shall be in possession\nof valid proof of training as required in the portfolio of evidence of the contractor.\n1.3.7.3 Awareness & Promotion\nThe Contractor is required to have scheme in place to promote an Occupational Health & Safety\nawareness and culture in employees. The following are some of the methods that may be used:\n• Toolbox Talks\n• Occupational Health & Safety Posters\n• Videos\n• Competitions\n• Suggestion schemes\n• Participative activities such as Occupational Health & Safety circles.\n1.3.7.4 Competence\nThe Contractor shall ensure that all appointed staff is competent and that all training required to\ndo the work safely and without risk to health, has been completed before work commences.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that follow-up and refresher training is conducted as the contract work\nprogresses and the work situation change. Records of all training shall be kept on the Health &\nSafety file for auditing purposes.\n1.3.8 Consultation, Communication and Liaison\nOccupational Health & Safety Liaison between the Client, Principal Contractor, other Contractors,\nDesigner and other concerned parties will be through the Client/Project Manager. In addition to\nthe above, communication may be directly with the Client or his appointed Agent, verbally or in\nwriting, as and when the need arises.\nThe Principal Contractor will be required to do Site Safety Audits with the Client/Project Manager\non a basis to be determined between the two parties.\n1.3.9 Checking, Reporting and Corrective Actions\n3.3.9.1 Monthly Audit by Client (Construction Regulation 4(1)(d)\nThe Client or his agent will conduct minimum monthly audits to comply with Construction\nRegulation 5(1) (o) to ensure that the Contractor has implemented and is maintaining the agreed\nand approved Occupational Health & Safety Plan.\n\nThe Contractor is to conduct his own minimum monthly internal audits to verify compliance with\nhis own Occupational Health & Safety plan.\nThe Occupational Health & Safety Representative is to conduct monthly inspections of their areas\nof responsibility and report thereon to their supervisor\nAll the results of the abovementioned inspections shall be in writing, reviewed, endorsed and\nplaced on the Occupational Health & Safety File.\n1.3.10 Incident Reporting and Investigation\n1.3.10.1 Reporting of Accidents and Incidents\nThe Contractor shall report all incidents where an employee is injured on duty to the extent that\nhe/she:\n• dies\n• becomes unconscious\n• loses a limb or part of a limb\n• is injured or becomes ill to such a degree that he/she is likely either to die or to suffer a\npermanent physical defect or likely to be unable for a period of at least 14 days either to\nwork or continue with the activity for which he/she was usually employed\nOR where:\n• a major incident occurred\n• the health or safety of any person was endangered\n• where a dangerous substance was spilled\n• the uncontrolled release of any substance under pressure took place\n• machinery or any part of machinery fractured or failed resulting in flying, falling or\nuncontrolled moving objects\n• machinery ran out of control\nto the Client and to the Provincial Director of the Department of Labour forthwith (Section 24 of\nthe Act & General Administrative Regulation 8.)\nThe Contractor is required to provide the Client with copies of all internal and external\naccident/incident investigation as well as all statutory reports required in terms of the Act within 7\ndays of the incident occurring.\n1.3.10.2 Accident and Incident Investigation\nThe Contractor is responsible for the investigation of all accidents/incidents where employees and\nnon-employees were injured to the extent that he/she/they had to be referred for medical\ntreatment by a doctor, hospital or clinic and the results of the investigation shall be entered into\nthe Accident/Incident Register.\nThe Contractor is responsible for the investigation of all minor, non-injury incidents and near\nmisses. The Client reserves the right to hold its own investigation into an incident or call for an\nindependent external investigation.\n1.3.11 Operational Control\n1.3.11.1 Emergency Preparedness, Contingency Planning and Response\nThe Contractor shall appoint a competent person to act as Emergency Coordinator.\nThe Contractor shall conduct an emergency identification exercise and establish what\nemergencies could possibly develop. He/she must then develop detailed contingency plans and\nemergency procedures.\n\n1.3.11.2 First Aid\nThe Contractor shall provide relevant First Aid equipment and have qualified First Aider/s on site\nas required by General Safety Regulation 3 of the Occupational Health & Safety Act.\n1.3.11.3 Security\nThe Contractor shall develop, implement and maintain Security- and Site Access Control rules\nand procedures throughout the construction period. Access control shall include the rule that non-\nemployees will not be allowed on site unaccompanied.\n1.3.11.4 Fall Protection (Working in Elevated Positions)\nAny work undertaken at height above ground level higher than two metres or any floor level will\nbe classified as "Work in Elevated Positions" and a pre-emptive Risk Assessment shall be carried\nout.\nWorkers working in elevated positions shall be trained to do this safely, without risk and compliant\nwith legislation.\nRisk Assessment shall take the possibility into account of persons falling through fragile material,\nskylights and other openings in the roof.\n1.3.11.5 Structures\nThe Contractor shall ensure that:\n• Steps are taken to ensure that no structure becomes unstable or collapses due to\nconstruction work being performed on it or in the vicinity of it\n• No structure is overloaded to the extent where it becomes unsafe\n• He/she has received from the designer the following information:\n• Information on known or anticipated hazards relating to the construction work and the\nrelevant information required for the safe execution of the construction work.\n• A geo-scientific report (where applicable)\n• The loading the structure is designed to bear\n• The methods and sequence of the construction process\n• All drawings pertaining to the design are on site and available for inspection\n1.3.11.6 Temporary Works\nTemporary work shall be carried out under the supervision of a competent person designated in\nwriting to do so.\nAll drawings pertaining to the temporary work shall be kept available on site. A competent person\nshall check all equipment used in the erection of temporary work before it is used.\n1.3.11.7 Access Scaffolding\nAccess Scaffolding shall be erected, used and maintained safely in accordance with Construction\nRegulation 16 and SA Bureau of Standards Code of Practice, SANS 085 entitled, "The Design,\nErection, Use & Inspection of Access Scaffolding.\nDetailed consideration shall be given to all scaffolding to ensure that it is properly planned to meet\nthe working requirements.\nScaffolding may only be erected, altered or dismantled by a person who has adequate training\nand experience in this type of work or under the supervision of such a person (Proof of\ncompetence to be put on the OHS File).\n1.3.11.8 Construction Vehicles & Mobile Plant (CV&MP)\nAll Construction Vehicles and Mobile Plant shall be inspected by the Contractor prior to being\nallowed on a project site and suppliers of hired vehicles, plant and equipment will be required to\ncomply with this specification as well as the Occupational Health & Safety Act and Regulations.\n\nNo unauthorised persons are to be allowed to drive CV&MP. Operators/drivers of CV&MP shall\nbe competent to operate the equipment safely and be in possession of a valid medical certificate\nissued by an Occupational Medicine Practitioner testifying that the holder is physically and\npsychologically fit to operate the equipment.\n1.3.11.9 Electrical Installations\nTemporary electrical installations shall be carried out by competent persons, and controlled by a\ncompetent person that has been appointed to do so in writing, in accordance with Construction\nRegulation 24 and the Electrical Installation Regulations. Temporary electrical installations shall\nbe inspected at least once per week by a competent person and a record of the inspections kept\nin the Occupational Health & Safety File.\nThe Contractor shall ensure that:\n• existing electrical services are located and marked before construction commences and\nduring the progress thereof. Where this is not possible, workers with jackhammers etc. are\nto be protected against electric shock by the use of suitable protective equipment like\ninsulated handles, rubber mats etc.\n• electrical installations and -machinery are sufficiently robust to withstand working conditions\non site.\n• all electrical machinery used on site are inspected before start-up on a daily basis by a\ncompetent person and that a record of the inspection is kept in the Occupational Health &\nSafety File.\nAn electrical and mechanical lock-out procedure for the construction site shall be developed by\nthe Principal Contractor and submitted for approval by the Project Manager before construction\ncommences. This lock-out procedure shall be adhered to by all Contractors on site.\n1.3.11.10 Housekeeping\nThe Contractor shall ensure that good housekeeping practises are implemented so that:\n• an unimpeded work space is maintained for every employee.\n• the walls and roof of every indoor workplace is sound and leak-free.\n• every workplace is kept clean, orderly and free of tools and materials that is not required\nfor the work being done.\n• every floor, walkway, stair, passage and gangway is kept in a good state of repair, skid-\nfree and free of obstruction, waste and materials.\n• catch platforms or -nets are erected over entry and exit ways or over places where\npersons are working to prevent them being struck by falling objects.\n• openings in floors, hatchways, stairways and open sides of floors or buildings are\nbarricaded, fenced, boarded over or provided with protection to prevent persons from\nfalling through or off them.\n• materials and equipment are stored properly.\n• materials ready for use is placed safely and not allowed to accumulate or cause an\nobstruction to pedestrian and vehicular traffic.\n• Scrap, waste and debris is removed regularly and in a safe manner.\n• construction sites are fenced off to prevent entry by unauthorised persons.\n\n1.3.11.11 Eating-, Changing-, Washing- and Toilet Facilities\nEating facilities should be provided in a location that is sheltered from the elements.\nAdequate changing-, washing – and toilet facilities shall be provided for both sexes.\nAt least 1 shower per 15 workers and 1 toilet per 30 workers shall be provided. Chemical toilets\nmay be used instead of the water borne sewerage type.\n1.3.11.12 Personal & Other Protective Equipment\nThe Contractor shall identify the hazards in the workplace and endeavour to eliminate them.\nWhere this is not possible, suitable steps shall be taken to protect workers from these hazards.\nEngineering- and other solutions to mitigate the hazard(s) should be attempted before the issue\nof personal protective equipment (PPE) is considered.\nThe Contractor is required to inform employees of health and safety hazards and issue them with\nsuitable equipment to protect them from these hazards. It is a further requirement that the\nContractor maintains the equipment and instructs and train employees in the use of the\nequipment. Employees do not have the right to refuse to use/wear safety equipment.\n1.3.11.13 Portable Electrical Tools & Equipment\nPortable electrical tools and equipment is defined as units taking electrical power from 220Volt 15\nAmp power outlets and is moved around the workplace to perform work like drilling, sawing,\ngrinding etc. and also include portable lights. Electrical appliances, on the other hand, include\nitems like fridges, stoves and heaters.\n1.3.11.14 Public Health & Safety\nThe Contractor is responsible for ensuring that non-employees affected by the construction work,\nlike visitors, the surrounding community and passers-by, are made aware of the dangers likely to\narise from the construction work as well as the precautionary measures to be observed to avoid\nor minimise these dangers. Appropriate signage must be posted to this effect and all employees\non site shall be instructed to ensure that non-employees are protected at all times. All non-\nemployees entering the site must receive induction into the hazards and risks and the control\nmeasures.\n\nAnnexure “A”\nCONSTRUCTION OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH - SAFETY – RISK ASSESSMENT\nDenotes items applicable to both Construction sites and Contractors Plant/Storage Yards\nELEMENT REMARKS\n1. Administrative & Legal Requirements • Dept. of labour will be notified – Annexure 2\n• Updated copy of OHS Act will be available on site\n• All legally required appointments will be made as specified in the OHS Act and\nConstruction Regulations\n• Site specific health and safety specification will be drawn up and provided to all\nprospective tendering contractors\n• Site specific risk assessment will be conducted and monitored and reviewed on\na regular basis\n• Written proof of registration and good standing of contractor with COID will be\nobtained\n• Health and safety committee will be established and meetings conducted\n• All contractors will be required to enter into a Section 37(2) agreement\n• All incidents/accidents will be reported and investigated as required\n• Detailed and site-specific fall protection plan will be drawn up and implemented\n– Employees fitness to work at heights will be determined and records kept\n• Cherry pickers will be load tested and valid load test certificates will be kept on\nfile – Regular safety inspections by competent persons done and records kept\n• Only persons medically tested in the form of Annexure 3 of the Construction\nRegulations and declared medically fit for the type of construction work to be\ndone will be allowed to work on site\n• All excavations will be inspected by a competent person before every shift as\nrequired, edges will be sloped to at least the angle of repose, the excavations\nwill be substantially barricaded and egress will be provided at least every 6\nmetres\n• Demolition work will be carried out under the supervision of a competent person,\ndetailed and site specific risk assessment will be carried out and engineering\nsurvey and method statement will be available on site\n\n• Inspections to prevent premature collapse will be carried out by competent\nperson before each shift. Inspection register kept\n• Cranes/Lifting Machines & equipment will be operated under the supervision of\na competent person\n• Valid load test certificates and 3 monthly inspection records of all lifting tackle\nwill be kept on site\n• Emergency and fire protection plan will be drawn up and displayed, emergency\nteams trained and available\n• All the legally required first aid equipment will be provided and clearly located -\nTrained and qualified first aiders available on site\n• Assessment will be conducted to determine the personal protective equipment\nrequirements, all equipment issued free of charge and the wearing of the\nequipment will be strictly enforced\n• Gas welding/cutting equipment only used by competent persons and equipment\nplaced on register and inspected regularly to ensure its safety\n• Alphabetical list of all chemicals on site will be drawn up and material safety\ndata sheets for all hazardous substances obtained – first aiders will be trained in\nthe correct first aid measures to be taken in case of injury or illness caused by\nhazardous chemicals\n• All construction vehicles will be inspected daily before start up and only operated\nby operators who are competent and medically fit to do so\n1. Education, Training & Promotion • All employees entering the site will be required to undergo a site specific\nhealth and safety induction training programme – A copy of the site rules\nwill also be made available to them\n• Relevant employees will receive specific safety training such a training in\nthe safe work procedures for plant, equipment and substances they are\nrequired to use\n• All visitors to the site will be given induction training and will only be\nallowed on the site if they are accompanied by a member of the site staff\n• Specific training will include inter alia first aid training, general safety\ntraining, firefighting training, operator training\n\n1. Public Safety & Emergency • Signage will be used to limit access to the site – “No unauthorised entry”,\n“Visitors to report to site office” and other relevant signage will be used\nPreparedness\n• General signage warning of overhead work and other hazards on site will be\ndeployed\n• Netting or other measures will be used to protect persons from falling\nobjects\n• Security measures such as patrols to prevent unauthorised entry as well as\nan entry register will placed in use\n1. Personal Protective Equipment • Assessment will be conducted to determine the personal protective equipment\nrequired on site\n• All equipment will be issued free of charge and the wearing thereof strictly\nenforced – this will also count for visitors to the site\n1. Housekeeping • Removal of rubble will form part of the project – Rubble to be crushed and\nremoved by truck to predetermined dump site\n• High standards of housekeeping will be enforced on all contractors\n1. Scaffolding, Formwork & Support work All legal requirements to be addressed and adhered to\n2. Ladders All legal requirements to be addressed and adhered to\n3. Electrical Safeguarding All legal requirements to be addressed and adhered to\n4. Emergency/Fire Prevention & Protection • Sufficient firefighting equipment will be provided, correctly located and clearly\nsignposted\n• Emergency plan will be formulated for evacuation and published\n1. Excavations & Demolition • Demolition work will be carried out under the supervision of a competent person,\ndetailed and site specific risk assessment will be carried out and engineering\nsurvey and method statement will be available on site\n• Inspections to prevent premature collapse will be carried out by competent person\nbefore each shift. Inspection register kept\n• All excavations will be inspected by a competent person before every shift as\nrequired, edges will be sloped to at least the angle of repose, the excavations well\nbe substantially barricaded and egress will be provided at least every 6 metres\n\n• All excavations will be inspected by a competent person before every shift as\nrequired, edges will be sloped to at least the angle of repose, the excavations will\nbe substantially barricaded and egress will be provided at least every 6 metres\n1. Tools • All hand tools will be in good condition and will be inspected regularly for safety –\nFindings will be entered into a register kept for this purpose\n1. Cranes • Cranes/Lifting Machines & equipment will be operated under the supervision of a\ncompetent person\n• Valid load test certificates and 3 monthly inspection records of all lifting tackle will\nbe kept on site\n1. Personnel & Material Hoists • Cherry pickers will be load tested and valid load test certificates will be kept on file\n– Regular safety inspections by competent persons done and records kept – Only\npersons medically tested for physical and psychological fitness and declared\ncompetent will be allowed to work on cherry pickers\n1. Transport & Materials Handling • All construction vehicles will be inspected daily before start up and only operated\nby operators who are competent and medically fit to do so\n1. Site Plant & Machinery • All construction vehicles will be inspected daily before start up and only operated\nby operators who are competent and medically fit to do so\n1. Plant & Storage Yards/Site Workshops Specifics • Good housekeeping practices and environmental protection to be practiced as far\nas is reasonably practicable\n1. Health & Hygiene • All hygiene facilities such as toilets, eating areas, change rooms and the like will\nbe provided in line with the Facilities Regulations and the Construction\nRegulations and will be kept in a clean and hygienic condition\n\n1.
