recommendation\n4\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n1. INTRODUCTION\nOn the 26th March 2021, COGHSTA appointed Iceburg Trading 751cc to undertake the Geotechnical\ninvestigation for the proposed 100 low cost housing located in Nababeep Village, Northern Cape\nProvince. The physical and geotechnical nature of the site needs to be investigated to facilitate the civil\nengineering design and foundation solution. Iceburg Trading 751cc was advised that the development\ncomprises of a 100-infill low cost housing to be developed in Nababeep area.\nThe report presents the finding of the site with regards to the Revised Edition of the Guide to Soil\nProfiling for Civil Engineering Purposes in Southern Africa which was published in September 2002.\nThis report discusses the method of investigation, geotechnical conditions encountered with on-site\nmaterial characteristics, recommendations, and general considerations.\nThe level of information provided in this report is deemed suitable for planning purposes.\n1. PURPOSE OF INVESTIGATION AND SCOPE OF WORK\nThe scope of work comprises trial pits excavation and soil sampling within the proposed areas/villages,\nthis includes:\n• Determine the geological origin of the material on site.\n• Identify and discuss the main on-site geotechnical constraints;\n• Materials classification according to TRH14 of different in-situ materials (indicator tests – mod\nand CBR related – in situ densities and in situ CBR) for suitability in re-use in new layer works\nand fill below walkways.\n• Identify problem areas at a sufficiently early stage.\n• Determining the ground water depth level, along the soggy areas\n• Identify risk and consequence of failure.\n• Identify and discuss the main on-site geotechnical constraints;\n• Recommendation on aspects of the site that can adversely affect the design and cost of the\nproject (Facilitate an adequate and economical design).\n• Comment on the founding conditions\n• Provide the geotechnical basis for planning and preliminary design purposes.\nThe scope of work was accomplished by:\n• Excavation of 157 trial holes to maximum depth of 1.6m below ground level by Bell TLB;\n• Performing laboratory tests to determine physical or engineering characteristics of the soil\nonsite;\n5\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n• Performing engineering analysis to develop design guidelines and recommendations.\nSubsequent sections of this report contain descriptions of the fieldwork, general subsurface conditions,\ndesign recommendations, and construction considerations.\n1. CHALLENGES\nThe proposed scope was to excavate a minimum of two trial holes per stand as per the guideline, however\nthe investigation was within the build-up area, some investigated stands are occupied by houses and\nunderground services. The challenges encountered was that some of the stand didn’t have enough space\nto excavate two trial pits because of the existing house, hence one trial pit was excavated. Two open\nportions were also investigated as per ‘SANS 634:2012 Geotechnical investigation for township\ndevelopment’ and ‘Generic Specification GFSH-2:2002 Geotechnical site investigation for housing\ndevelopment’.\n1. AVAILABLE INFORMATION\n• A 1:250 000 scale Geological Map from Council for Geoscience\n• Topographical maps and google earth images provided by the client\n• A field walkover survey done by the consultant and the municipality.\n1. METHOD OF INVESTIGATION\nThe geotechnical investigation commenced with a desktop study, which entailed obtaining as much\ninformation as possible of the site that may provide indication of the most likely sub-soil and associated\ngeotechnical conditions prevailing within the area. By determining the underlying geological setting\ntogether with prevailing topographical and climatic condition, the weathering characteristic of the host\nrock can be estimated and the indication of the most likely geotechnical condition underlying the site\ncan be established.\nThe desktop study was followed by a site reconnaissance which was carried on 16th May 2021. This was\ndone through walking over the entire area whilst noting and recording information for visible surface\ninformation, from this phase of investigation together with desktop study, provided the preliminary\nassessment of the geotechnical condition underlying the site and identify areas necessary for further\ninvestigation.\nField investigation was carried out on 17 to 23rd May 2021 and entailed setting out and excavating 157\ntrial holes using Tlb (Tractor loader backhoe). Holes referred as TP 01 to TP 157 were excavated at\nposition shown on the attached plan.\n6\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nRepresentative soil samples were sampled from the soil horizons and submitted to soil laboratory for\ntesting. The following tests were conducted.\n• Foundation Indicator tests\n• MOD AASHTO compaction and CBR\n• Material Classification\n• pH and Conductivity\n1. BIOPHYSICAL DESCRIPTION\n6.1 SITE LOCATION AND DESCRIPTION\nThe study area is Nababep Village, Springbok, Northern Cape Province. Nababeep is located 7,9km north\nof Springbok town. The investigated village lies on the eastern side on the National Road 7 (N7). The\ninvestigated area is an existing residential village with houses and shacks, and it is subdivided into sub-\nvillages. Municipality is supplying Water and electricity in bulk to the existing village. Electricity supply\nis by means of an overhead network.\nThe vegetation comprises of trees, shrubs and few outcrops of granites, mainly foliated and quartzites\nwere encountered during the walkover survey and fieldwork.\n7\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nFigure 6-1: A Photograph showing outcrops found onsite\nTable 1: Coordinates of the proposed investigated village\nLatitude 29°35'31.04"S\nLongitude 17°52'33.44"E\n8\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nFigure 6-2: Locality map, showing the investigated village.\n9\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nFIGURE 6-3 TIAL PITS LOCATION\n10\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n6.2 SURFACE WATER\nNo streams and water ponds were noted during the walkover survey over the proposed area for\ninvestigation.\n6.3 LAND USE\nThe investigated area is an existing residential village with houses and shacks, with services such as\nwater and electricity from the municipality to the existing village.\n1. GEOLOGY\nAccording to the 1:250 000 geological map the investigated area is expected to be underlain by different\ntypes of Quartzites, Granite and Granite Gneiss of the Namaqualand Suite.\n11\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nFigure 7-1: Geology map of the investigated area\n12\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n1. CLIMATE AND HYDROLOGY\nNababeep normally receives about 117mm of rain per year, with most rainfall occurring during summer.\nIt receives the lowest rainfall (0mm) in January and the highest (25mm) in June. The average midday\ntemperatures for Nababeep range from 16.5°C in July to 28.3°C in February. The region is the coldest\nduring July when the mercury drops to 3.8°C on average during the night.\nWeinert (1964), through his work on basic rocks in Southern Africa, demonstrated that mechanical\ndisintegration is the predominant mode of rock weathering in areas where his climatic “N-value” is\ngreater than 5, while chemical decomposition predominates where the N-value is less than 5. The\nclimatic N-value of the area is <5, which implies that chemical decomposition is the dominant mode or\nrock weathering.\n13\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nFIGURE 8-1 QUARTERNARY CATCHMENT MAP\n14\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n1. NATURE OF INVESTIGATION\n9.1 TRIAL PITS\nThe site investigation was carried out on the 17th to the 23rd May 2021. Hundred and fifty-seven trial pits\nwere excavated with a backhoe loader. The maximum depth reached is 1.6m below ground level. The\ntrial pits were internally profiled by a registered Engineering Geologist in accordance with the latest\nstandard of South Africa (after Jennings et. al. 1973).\nDisturbed samples were selectively retrieved in order to determine the soil grading, compaction\ncharacteristics and general material properties. The samples were submitted to an SANAS accredited\nlaboratory. The trial pit was loosely backfilled after profiling.\nThe positions of the trial pits were determined with the aid of a hand-held GPS, the test pit profile is\npresented in Appendix A of this report and its approximate location is indicated on a test pits locality\nplan attached in Appendix D.\nFigure 9-1: A photograph showing a TLB machine excavating on site\n15\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL 157\n9.2 SAMPLING METHODS AND MATERIAL TESTING\nThe investigation was further supplemented by limited materials sampling and testing. Representative\nsamples were taken to the lab and the following tests performed:\n• Indicator Test: samples were collected from various layers exposed in the trial pits and\nsubmitted to the lab for sieve analysis, atterberg limits (LS, PI, LL).\n• California Bearing Ratio (CBR): samples were collected from the soil material and submitted to\na lab for analysis. The test results are used as an important input to soil classification using USCS\nand AASHTO classification systems for soil description and correlation. This test is done to\ndetermine the quality and suitability of in-situ materials for use in earthworks.\n• Chemical soil aggressiveness testing: To determine the proneness of the in-situ material to\ncorrosivity with specific reference to underground services (stormwater, water reticulation and\nsewer pipes and electrical cables).\n1. SUBSURFACE SOIL CONDITIONS\nThe geotechnical investigation revealed that the profile across the site is uniform, comprising of the\nfollowing horizons:\n• Colluvium;\n• Bedrock\nThese horizons are described in more detail below:\n10.1.1 COLLUVIUM\nThe colluvium soil horizon forms the upper most layers of the site. It is characterised by slightly moist,\nlight to reddish brown, orange red to grey, loose to medium dense, intact, sandy silty gravel and gravely\nsandy silty and few abundant sub-rounded and sub-angular cobbles of granite were noted clast supported\nin a matrix of reddish brown in some area.\nThis horizon varies in thickness across the site. The material found here are loose, unconsolidated\nsediments that have been deposited either by gravity or downslope creep, or a variable combination of\n23\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nthese processes. This horizon was underlain by the bedrock which is expected to be granite gneiss and\nquartzites.\n10.1.2 BEDROCK\nThe granite gneiss bedrock on site occurs as moderately to highly weathered, closely jointed, black and\ngrey, medium grained and the hardness of the rock was generally profiled as medium hard to hard rock.\nThe minimum thickness of the bedrock is approximately 0.55m. Excavation was not possible due to the\nbedrock outcropping at some portions of the site.\n24\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n1. Exp. = Potential Expansiveness (vd Merwe Classification) USCS\n= Unified Soil Classification System AASHTO – American Association for State Highways and Transport Officials.\n29\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nOne of the major design factors for building structures is the shrinking and swelling potential of fine-\ngrained soils. The shrink/swell movements can be estimated using the Plasticity Index (PI). Generally,\nthe higher the PI of a material, the greater the potential for soil movements during moisture changes;\nthe results of the Atterberg Limits tests indicate that the soil at the site has low expansion potential\n(Effective PI = 12).\nShrink-swell movement occurs in response to soil moisture content changes beneath the slab.\nMoisture changes occur beneath the slab due to seasonal changes in the relative amount of rainfall\nand evaporation potential. Also, soil moisture changes result from the construction of a slab-on-grade\nfloor due to the presence of the slab/vapor barrier/grade beam system. The installation of the slab,\nvapor barrier, and grade beams reduces the natural moisture transfer from the subsurface beneath\nthe building and generally causes the soil moisture content to increase in the soil beneath the building\nafter construction. Another significant cause of soil moisture change is changes in vegetation,\nparticularly trees, associated with landscaping.\nMaterial on-site is classified as SC, SW and SM and class in accordance to Unified Soil Classification\nSystem (USCS). SC is clayey sands, sand- clay mixtures with Atterberg limits above A- line with PI >\n7; SW is well-graded sands, gravelly sands (little or no fines); SM is silty sands, sand-silt mixtures,\nAtterberg limits below A- line or PI < 4.\nA summary of laboratory results is presented in Appendix B of this report. The laboratory test results\nindicate that the site is typically underlain by gravelly sand and gravel silt underlain by bedrock. The\nLiquid Limit of the samples ranges between 0% and 28% with the Linear Shrinkage ranging between\n0% and 6.5%. The samples indicate non to low plasticity with the Plasticity Index of the samples\nranging between NP and 12%. Furthermore, the laboratory tests indicate that the samples have high\nsilty sand and gravelly sand content. The low Plasticity Index indicate that the samples will exhibit a\nnon to low potential expansiveness (Van der Merwe, 1964).\n30\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nTable 4: Summary of CBR test results\nCBR @ Max. 2021\nCBR tests carried out on material or soil from the site are classified as G5 to G9 gravel soil in\naccordance with the COLTO: 1998.\nMaterial was sampled and classified as fair to good graded silty sand to gravel sand with a grading\nmodulus of between 1.1 and 2.5. Soils with a good grading are generally characterized with higher\ngrading modulus. Coarse grained soil has some good compatibility characteristics compared to finer\nmaterial and can normally carry much heavier loads without deformation than finer materials. The\nCBR samples indicate low plasticity with the Plasticity Index ranging between NP and 12% which may\nbe associated with non to low expansive potential according to Van Der Merwe’s Classification\nSystem. Soil found at this site has fair to good compatibility characteristics and therefore as such\nmaterial on-site can be used as sub-grade and selected layers during construction stage\n34\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nTable 5: Summary of soil pH and conductivity\nSample No. Depth (m) pH Conductivity (s.m-1)\nTP17 0.0 – 0.2 8.33 @ 21.7°C 0.05 @ 21.9°C\nTP25 0.0 – 0.7 7.95 @ 21.6°C 0.08 @ 21.7°C\nTP31 0.0 – 0.3 7.94 @ 21.6°C 0.07 @ 22.0°C\nTP128 0.0 – 0.7 8.28 @ 21.6°C 0.71 @ 21.3°C\nTP148 0.0 – 0.4 7.94 @ 21.7°C 0.10 @ 22.0°C\n35\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nChemical test of soils has a pH value ranging between 7.94 to 8.33. The pH values may be used as an\ninitial indicator of potential corrosion problems, since bio-corrosion occurs optimally in the pH ranges\nof 2-6, while chemical attack generally occurs in either highly alkaline or highly acidic condition.\nTherefore, the results indicate that the soil is slightly acid to slightly alkaline with a conductivity ranging\nbetween 0.05 to 0.71. Conductivity is used as an initial indication of electrochemical corrosion potential.\n1. GROUND WATER CONDITIONS\nGroundwater seepage was not encountered in any of the trial pits excavated on the site. Problems due\nto ground water seepage are therefore expected in places, especially during and after a very wet rainy\nseason.\nMaximum depth reached was at 1.6m depth below ground level with no sign of near surface water\ntable as such the water table in the area is permanently deeper than 1.6 m below surface level.\n1. GEOTECHNICAL ASSESSMENT\nIt is also the purpose of this investigation to consider various geotechnical factors at the site which may\nhave an influence on the proposed developments and associated infrastructure. The geotechnical factors\nobserved during the field investigation are discussed below.\n13.1 PROBLEM SOILS\nProblem soils such as dolomite and marshy areas were not encountered on site.\n13.2 SLOPE STABILITY\nLayers for material encountered in all trial pits were stable during excavation and profiling, hence,\nslope instability is not anticipated over the area.\n13.3 HEAVE AND HIGH COMPRESSIBILITY\nThis will affect concrete structures around the site. It is recommended that during construction,\nshould clay be encountered alongside or below any structures, it should be removed and replaced with\nsuitable material with no heave or high compressibility potential.\n13.4 EXISTENCE OF COLLAPSIBLE/COMPRESSIBLE PROFILE\nThe soils encountered on the site typically comprise silty sand and gravely sand underlain by bedrock\nwith no visual open-textured structure such as pinholes which indicate collapse potential. However, silty\nsand and gravelly sand have been documented to exhibit low to moderate collapse potential. From the\nsite observations, it is anticipated that the site has low to moderate collapse potential. Compressible\nsoils typically comprise fine grained soils such as clay, clayey sand and clayey silt with low plasticity,\ngravelly and sandy soil. The soils underlying the area are silty sand and gravelly sand underlain by\n36\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nbedrock, therefore problems associated with compressible soils such as differential settlement is not\nanticipated on the site.\n13.5 EXPANSIVE SOILS\nThe site is mainly underlain by silty sand and gravelly sand underlain by bedrock, therefore, problems\nassociated with expansive soils such as differential volume change of the soil (heaving and shrinking)\nare not anticipated on the site.\n13.6 ERODIBILITY OF THE SOIL\nDuring the site investigation, visible signs of surface soil erosion were not identified, in the form of\nerosion gullies and rills to indicate that the material could be erodible. The risk of erosion on the\nproposed site is not expected.\n13.7 EXCAVATION CLASSIFICATION WITH RESPECT TO SERVICES\nTrial pits were excavated to depths ranging between 0.1 and 1.6 mbgl with all of the test pits terminated\ndue to refusal at hard dig or bedrock. Excavations for the proposed development are expected to utilise\nsoft excavation techniques to remove the thin layer of colluvium material and hardrock excavation\ntechniques to remove the bedrock. It is recommended that all required earthworks be carried out in\naccordance with guidelines provided by SANS 1200 (latest edition).\nTable 6 Classification of material for machine excavation (SANS 1200D\nCLASSIFICATION DESCRIPTION\nRestricted excavation\nSoft Material which can be efficient removed by a back-acting excavator having a\nfly wheel power > 0.10 kW for each mm of tined bucket width.\nIntermediate Material which can be efficient removed by a back-acting excavator having a\nfly wheel power > 0.10 kW for each mm of tined-bucket width or with the use\nof pneumatic tools before removal by a machine capable of removing soft\nmaterial.\nHard rock Material that cannot be removed without blasting or wedging and splitting.\nNon-restricted excavation\nSoft Material which can be efficiently removed or loaded, without prior ripping, by\nany of the following plant:\nA bulldozer or a track type front end loader having an approximate mass of 22\ntonne and a fly wheel power of 145 kW.\nA tractor-scrapper unit having an approximate mass of 28 tonne and fly wheel\npower of 245 kW, pushed during loading by a bulldozer equivalent to that\ndescribed above.\n37\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nIntermediate Material which can be efficiently ripped by a bulldozer having an approximate\nmass of 35 tonne and a fly wheel power of 220 kW.\nHard rock Material which cannot be efficiently ripped by a bulldozer having an\napproximate mass of 35 tonne and a fly wheel power of 220 kW.\nBoulder class A Material containing more than 40% by volume of boulders of size between 0.03\nm3 and 20 m3, in a matrix of soft material or smaller boulders.\nBoulder class B Material containing 40% or less by volume of boulders of size between 0.03 m3\nand 20 m3, in a matrix of soft material or smaller boulders.\nAll trial pits were excavated with Backhoe Loader. Based on engineering properties of material\nunderlying the proposed area; material on-site is classified soft to hard rock excavation (SANS 1200 D).\n13.8 GROUNDWATER CONDITIONS\nNo groundwater was encountered during test pits excavation or profiling. Maximum depth reached\nwas at 1.6m depth below ground level with no sign of near surface water table as such the water\ntable in the area is permanently deeper than 1.6 m below surface level.\n1. UNDERGROUND SERVICES\nThe investigations were conducted within a built-up area with services such as water supply, sewage\nsystem running along the street.\n1. DEVELOPMENT RECOMMENDATIONS\n15.1 PROPOSED DEVELOPMENT\nIt is understood that the investigation is for the development of 100 infill low-cost housing to be\ndeveloped in the Nababeep area.\n15.2 RIPPABILITY AND TRENCHABILITY\nSoft excavation in terms of SABS 1200 is anticipated to at least a depth of 0.6 m below existing ground\nlevel using light earthmoving equipment due to the soft nature of the underlying soils. Some hardrock\nexcavation may locally occur below 0.4 m where the bedrock occurs.\n15.3 EARTHWORK CONSTRUCTION\nIt is recommended that all earthworks be carried out along the guidelines given in SANS 1200 and should\nbe carried out in a manner to promote stable development of the site.\n38\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n15.4 SITE CLASSIFICATION\n39\nFor urban planning purpose the site is zoned according to the NHBRC classification systems. Due to the\npresence of colluvium horizons the entire site is classified as a Zone S1/C1/R site. The description of\nthis zoning is as follows:\nZone C1/S1: This zone covers a small portion of the site and is characterised by collapsible soils (fine\nsilts) with a total settlement movement between 5mm to 10mm and differential movement that is 75%\n(C1) and fine-grained soils (clayey sand) with a total settlement movement less than 10mm and\ndifferential movement that is 50% (S)\nZone R: This zone is characterised by the occurrence of bedrock at shallow depths. The bedrock (refusal)\nis encountered at depths shallower than 1 m.\n1. CONCLUSION\nBased on desktop study geotechnical considerations the area is suitable for the development. The\nfollowing must be taken into account:\n• Geology: Soils associated with the proposed site are mainly silty sands, gravelly sands and\nshallow bedrock.\n• Undermined Areas or Ground: The site has not been subject to underground mining.\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\n• Sinkholes and Sub-surface Subsidence: The geological conditions underlying the site do not lend\nthemselves to the formation of sinkholes or surface subsidence such as dolines.\n• The area is underlain by different types of Quartzite, Granite and Gneiss of the Namaqualand\nSuite.\n• The geotechnical investigation revealed that the profile across the site is uniform, comprising\nof the colluvium and bedrock\n• The low Plasticity Index indicate that the samples will exhibit a low potential expansiveness\n(Van der Merwe, 1964).\n• CBR tests carried out on material or soil from the site are classified as G5 to G9 gravel soil in\naccordance with the COLTO: 1998.\n• Material on-site are classified as SC, SW and SM and class in accordance to Unified Soil\nClassification System (USCS).\n• The granite gneiss bedrock on site occurs as moderately to highly weathered.\n1. RECOMMENDATIONS\nBased on the profiles of trial pits excavated within the area proposed for the development, together\nwith results of a laboratory tests, the following foundation can be suggested:\n• Normal foundation\n• Soil raft foundation\nThe foundation recommendation per stand is attached in Appendix D\n1. ADDITIONAL INVESTIGATIONS\nNo additional investigation is required.\n1. CONSTRUCTION MONITORING\n19.1 EXCAVATION INSPECTION\nThe excavation needs to be inspected by a competent person prior to placing any concrete.\n19.2 CONTROL TESTING\nRegular checks on the quality and compaction of the backfill to the terraces should be made.\n40\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nAppendices\n41\n\nMWEM21-12\nGEOTECHNICAL INVESTIGATION: NABABEEP, NORTHERN CAPE MAY 2021\nAppendix A: Soil\nProfiles\n42\n\nCoGHSTA LLLLEEEEGGGGEEEENNNNDDDD\nNababeep Housing Development SSSShhhheeeeeeeetttt 1111 ooooffff 1111\nJJOOBB NNUUMMBBEERR:: 000000\nBOULDERS {SA01}\nGRAVEL/gravels {SA02}\nGRAVELLY {SA03}\nSAND {SA04}\nSANDY {SA05}\nSILT {SA06}\nSILTY {SA07}\nCLAY {SA08}\nCLAYEY {SA09}\nQUARTZITE {SA15}\nPLUTONIC/norite/syenite {SA17}\nFREE QUARTZ/visible quartz {SA44}\nGRANITE {SA17}{SA44}\nName DISTURBED SAMPLE {SA38}\nCOBBLES {SA58}\nCONTRACTOR : INCLINATION : ELEVATION :\nMACHINE : DIAM : X-COORD :\nDRILLED BY : DATE : Y-COORD :\nPROFILED BY : DATE :\nLLLLEEEEGGGGEEEENNNNDDDD\nTYPE SET BY : DATE :06/07/2021 20:02\nSSSSUUUUMMMMMMMMAAAARRRRYYYY OOOOFFFF SSSSYYYYMMMMBBBBOOOOLLLLSSSS\nSETUP FILE :STANDARD.SET TEXT :..testpitlogsNababeep3.txt\nD0BE Mukona Consulting Engineers (Pty) Ltd dotPLOT 7022 PBpH67\n\nCoGHSTA HHHHOOOOLLLLEEEE NNNNoooo:::: TTTTPPPP1111\nNababeep Housing Development SSSShhhheeeeeeeetttt 1111 ooooffff 1111\nJJOOBB NNUUMMBBEERR:: 000000\nScale 0.00\n"Slightly moist, light brown, medium dense, intact, gravelly silty SAND\n1:10\nwith minor cobbles. NC/12/2026\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF 69 BNG HOUSES IN NABABEEP, NAMA-KHOI LOCAL\nMUNICIPALITY FOR THE NAMAKWA DISTRICT MUNICIPALITY\nC 4: SITE INFORMATION\nC 4.1 SCOPE\nThe documentation included describes the site as at the time of tender to enable the Tenderer to price\nhis tender and to decide upon his method of working and programming.\nWork will be executed in a residential area and the Contractor will take all necessary steps to ensure\nthe safety of people, animals and/or property.\nC 4.2 SUBSOIL INVESTIGATIONS, AND TEST RESULTS\nThe material on site varies. Soil on site is classified as C1/S/R and medium raft foundation is\nrecommended\nC 4.3 INFORMATION ABOUT PIPED AND OTHER SERVICES BELOW THE SURFACE OF THE SITE\nFOR CONTRACTS INVOLVING GROUND WORKS, AND ABOUT HOOK-UP AND BOUNDARY\nDETAILS FOR CONTRACTS WITH PLANT INTERFACES, IN ADDITION TO ANYTHING ABOUT\nTHE PHYSICAL SITE WHICH IMPACTS UPON THE CONTRACT\nAll existing services that could be indicated by the Employer are shown on the Drawings. The\nContractor will however investigate on site with the Engineer to identify any existing services that are\nnot indicated on the Drawings before any work commences in an area. As build drawings for sewer,\nwater, roads and electricity will be issued on commencement of the project\nPart C 4: Site Information C 4 - 1 C 4.1 - C 4.3\nTender Number: NC/12/2026\n\nVOLUME 2\n\nDRAWINGS\n\nDEPARTMENT OF CO-OPERATIVE GOVERNANCE, HUMAN SETTLEMENTS AND\nTRADITIONAL AFFAIRS OF THE NORTHERN CAPE\nTENDER NO. NC/12/2026\nTHE CONSTRUCTION OF 69 BNG HOUSES IN NABABEEP, NAMA-KHOI LOCAL\nMUNICIPALITY FOR THE NAMAKWA DISTRICT MUNICIPALITY\nDRAWINGS NOTES\nThe client accepts the fact that the contractor is a Professional builder and a master of his trade. It is\naccepted that he has visited the site and has familiarized himself with the local conditions before his tender\nwas submitted, to eliminate any possible chance that a misunderstanding might arise at a later stage due to\nhis limited knowledge of the site.\nAll work to be carried out strictly in accordance with the local authority building regulations and by-laws.\nAll materials to be SABS approved (stamped). Proof of which to be supplied (official certification) on request\nwhen applicable.\nWorkmanship to comply with the relevant National Building Regulations and Building Standards Act (Act 103\nof 1977 as amended) and the NHBRC home building Manual.\nFoundations to be inspected and approved by the engineer before concrete is poured. This also applies to\nservices in the ground.\nWall plate inspection and timber truss fixing to be approved before walls are plastered.\nTrade names specified are purely to indicate the quality and standard required. These can be substituted\nwith any product matching the quality subject to the engineer’s approval.\nThe contractor must check all the measurements and levels indicated on the drawings on site prior to the\nwork commencing.\nThe setting out of the building work remains the sole responsibility of the contractor.\nDo not scale the drawing. If uncertain about any of the dimensions, contact the Engineer.\nReport any discrepancies between the drawings and/or contract documentation to the Engineer prior to the\ncommencement of the work.\nAll the materials used must be of the best quality obtainable. The work must be done by skilled artisans and\ncarried out in such a way as to ensure the highest quality workmanship obtainable in the local industry. The\nstandard must at least be acceptable to members of the BIA and the M.B.A. Work not complying with afore\nsaid will be condemned and redone at the contractor’s expense.\nAll the materials, fittings and equipment used on the project must be applied/installed strictly according to the\nmanufacturer's specifications and guidelines.\nGreat care must be taken when installing the damp proof courses to ensure that no damaged material is\nused. All joints to have 150mm overlaps and to be taped.\nIt is important to note that the safety of any material brought onto the site and paid for by the client remains\nthe responsibility of the Contractor even though ownership has been transferred to the Client.\nAll materials on site must be stored properly to minimize any damage that might occur. Planning the work\nsequence is of the utmost importance when ordering materials to ensure that they are not stored for too long\na period.\nPlastered wall surfaces must be given sufficient time to dry out before any paintwork commences.\n\nThe contractor must insist that all site instructions be noted in writing in the site instruction book to avoid any\nlater disputes.\nContractor must familiarize himself with the 'Occupational Health and Safety act no. 85 of 1993' and fully\ncomply with the contents thereof. The safety of the public, visitors and of the workers on site is of the utmost\nimportance and remains his sole responsibility for the duration of the contract.\nThe site must be kept clean at all times and builder’s rubble removed from time to time Temporary toilet\nfacilities must be provided by the contractor for the workers and kept clean at all times.\nContractor must not deviate from the plans and must notify the Engineer of any problems immediately as\nthey arise.\nThis is to certify that I / we ………………………………………………………………………………………\nof (Tenderer) ………………………………………………………………………………………\n………………………………………………………………………………………\nof (Address) ………………………………………………………………………………………\n………………………………………………………………………………………\n………………………………………………………………………………………\nTelephone Number ………………………………………………………………………………………\nFax Number ………………………………………………………………………………………\non (Date) ………………………………………………………………………………………\nhave examined the drawing notes and its surroundings for which I/we am/are submitting this tender and\nhave, so far as is practicable, familiarized myself/ourselves with all the information, risks, contingencies and\nother circumstances which may influence or affect my/our tender.\nSIGNED ON BEHALF OF THE TENDERER: …………………………………………….…………………………\nSIGNED ON BEHALF OF THE CONSULTANT: ……………………………………………………………………\nDATE: ………………………………………………………...………\n\nPREPARATION OF BUILDING AREA\nRemove all organic material within 1.5m of perimeter of\nfoundation footprint.\nWet area to 1 - 2% of OMC (optimum moisture content).
